CONSTRAINT-SYSTEM FRAMEWORK Vocabulary, Definitions & Operating
Logic
This section reinterprets traditional market concepts into constraint-system language. Every operational section
that follows uses this vocabulary. Footnote references [1]–[13] map directly to the terms defined here.
FROM RETAIL LOGIC TO CONSTRAINT THINKING
Traditional retail trading assumes buyers vs. sellers create price movement, that trends persist, and that more indicators add edge.
This manual replaces that frame with constraint logic.
Traditional Term
Constraint-System Term
Operational Meaning
Market
Field
The entire operational environment — a system of constraints, not a
battleground.
Price
Resolution Output
The numeric output printed when the field resolves a constraint. Minimal
change required.
Trend
Direction of Constraint Resolution
Current orientation of the field's resolution process. Not assumed to persist.
Liquidity
Available Resolution Pathways
Channels through which the field resolves constraints. 'Liquidity grabs' =
pathway harvesting.
Breakout
Constraint Violation
Required when the field can no longer balance constraints with small
adjustments.
Pullback
Partial Rebalancing
Temporary adjustment after a strong move. Not a reversal — just short-term
rebalancing.
Stall
Resolution Output Stall
Field pauses to process a local constraint cluster. Precedes continuation or
reversal.
Stop / Stop-Loss
Constraint Boundary
Point at which the current constraint set is invalidated. Not arbitrary —
structurally defined.
Entry
Activation Signal
Defined condition showing a new constraint-resolution process has started.
P90 = one signal.
Fib Retracement Zone
Partial Rebalancing Range
Zone where the field rebalances by a measured proportion of the preceding
move.
200% Extension
Deep State
Resolution output extends far beyond daily mean variance. Often exhaustion
or last stage.
168% Extension
Stall Zone State
Region where resolution output frequently pauses. Field may continue or
begin rebalancing.
132% Extension
Kill-Switch State
Constraint set fully invalidated. Forces complete reevaluation of the resolution
process.
Small Asian Range
Constraint Deficit
Field is under-resolved. Must expand to reach daily mean variance — the
'coiled spring.'

=== PAGE 3 ===
CORE OPERATING PRINCIPLES
CONSTRAINT LOGIC — SIX OPERATING PRINCIPLES
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
1. THE FIELD IS NOT A BATTLEGROUND
   The field resolves conflicting intents through minimal changes (resolution outputs).
   There are no "buyers" or "sellers" — only constraint states being resolved.
2. NO INHERENT DIRECTION
   Direction of Constraint Resolution = the orientation of the current resolution process.
   It is not assumed to persist. Each session begins fresh with a new constraint set.
3. INDICATORS DO NOT CREATE INFORMATION
   Strong Subadditivity (SSA): the current state already embeds all past information.
   Adding more indicators does not increase edge — it increases noise.
4. BREAKOUTS ARE CONSTRAINT VIOLATIONS
   Resolution occurs when existing constraints cannot contain the state.
   The 132% level is the Kill-Switch State: the constraint set has been violated.
5. FIBONACCI LEVELS ARE STATES, NOT TARGETS
   168% = Stall Zone State (resolution output pausing at a constraint cluster)
   200% = Deep State (extended resolution, often exhaustion)
   132% = Kill-Switch State (invalidation of current constraint set)
6. TRADING IS STATE RECOGNITION, NOT CANDLE PREDICTION
   The Markov Property: the current state contains all necessary information.
   Past data is fully embedded in the present state. Align with the field's resolution.
CONSTRAINT-SYSTEM VOCABULARY GLOSSARY
Full footnote reference — these definitions apply throughout all Parts:
[1] Field
Replaces 'market.' The entire operational environment viewed as a system of constraints. Each resolution output is the minimal
adjustment that satisfies current constraints.
[2] Resolution Output
Replaces 'price.' The numeric output printed when the field resolves a constraint. Not an intrinsic value or an opinion — it is the minimal
change required.
[3] Direction of Constraint Resolution
Replaces 'trend.' The current orientation of the field's resolution process. Describes how the field is rebalancing constraints, not a
persistent direction.
[4] Available Resolution Pathways
Replaces 'liquidity.' The accessible channels through which the field resolves constraints. 'Liquidity grabs' become 'available resolution
pathway harvesting.'
[5] Constraint Violation

=== PAGE 4 ===
Replaces 'breakout.' A sudden change when the field's constraints are exceeded. Not random — required when the field can no longer
balance constraints with small adjustments.
[6] Partial Rebalancing
Replaces 'pullback.' Temporary adjustment after a strong movement. Indicates short-term resolution of an imbalance, not a reversal
signal.
[7] Resolution Output Stall
Replaces 'stall.' A period when the field temporarily stops advancing because it is processing a local constraint cluster. The
Stall-Harvest Trading System focuses on this phase.
[8] Constraint Boundary
Replaces 'stop' or 'stop-loss.' A state at which the current constraint set is invalidated. Not arbitrary — represents the limit beyond which
the resolution process must be reevaluated.
[9] Activation Signal
Replaces 'entry.' A defined condition showing that a new constraint-resolution process has started. The P90 candle is one such
activation signal.
[10] Fib Extension States
168%, 200%, 132% are not entries or stops. They are states describing how far the resolution output has extended. 168% = stall state;
200% = deep state; 132% = kill-switch state.
[11] Constraint Deficit and Expansion
Small Asian ranges act as a 'coiled spring.' When the field is under-resolved, it must expand to reach its daily mean variance.
[12] Strong Subadditivity (SSA)
Adding more indicators does not create new information. The current state already embeds all past information.
[13] Markov Property
The current state contains all necessary information for future probabilities. Past data is fully embedded in the present state; more
context does not add predictive power.
Constraint-System Framework | CEREBUS FX v4.0 | April 2026

1. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY: THE EDGE
The CEREBUS System exploits the statistical correlation between the Asian Session Range and the Daily Distribution.
Constraint Logic: Small Asian ranges create a Constraint Deficit [11]. The field is under-resolved and must expand to reach its daily
mean variance. The Asian Range defines the day's constraint set. Resolution Outputs [2] then satisfy that deficit.
Core Thesis: Small Asian ranges (<30 pips) act as a constraint deficit [11]. The field must expand to resolve it.
CFD Expansion Engine: Captures the initial resolution output expansion with tight constraint boundaries [8] and pyramided
activation signals [9].
Binary/Mean Reversion Engine: Capitalizes on available resolution pathway harvesting [4] at deep states (168%-200%) [10]
where the resolution output stalls [7] before reversing or continuing.

4. STRATEGY B: DEEP MEAN REBALANCING (The Resolution Output Stall Play)
This strategy monetizes the Resolution Output Stall [7] — where the resolution output taps a deep state (168%-200%) [10] via available
resolution pathway harvesting [4], then fails to continue, creating a high-probability partial rebalancing [6] opportunity.
A. The Logic

=== PAGE 7 ===
When the resolution output extends aggressively, it often reaches the Stall Zone (168%) [10] or Deep State (200%) [10] to harvest
available resolution pathways before rebalancing.
CFD Context: Usually a constraint boundary-out signal [8].
Binary/Mean Reversion Context: A high-probability activation signal [9] for a rebalancing snap-back.
B. The Setup
Trigger: Resolution output touches Stall Zone (168%) or Deep State (200%) [10] of the P90 candle.
Condition: Must occur before 12:00 PM EST.
Filter: The -50% Daily Target has NOT yet been hit.

VERDICT: Subsequent P90s in the SAME direction of constraint resolution [3] have HIGHER
win rates (87.8% vs 83.3%) and faster delivery (76 min vs 89 min).
This is a VALID cascade activation system [9].
SECTION 1: CASCADE ACTIVATION LOGIC DEFINED
CASCADE ACTIVATION DEFINITION
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
1. Initial P90 (Bias Setter):
   • First P90 candle in 2-11 AM EST window
   • Establishes direction of constraint resolution [3] for session
   • Standard Constraint Boundary [8]: 80% of P90 body
2. Cascade P90 (Momentum Confirmation):
   • Subsequent P90 candle in SAME direction of constraint resolution [3]
   • Must occur within 120 min of Initial P90
   • New Constraint Boundary [8]: 168% of THIS P90 body (wider — allows normal partial rebalancing [6])
   • Target: Same as Initial (-50% Asian Range constraint deficit resolution)
3. Maximum Cascades:
   • Max 3 cascade activations per session
   • Edge degrades after 3rd cascade

SECTION 9: COMPLETE CASCADE ACTIVATION PROTOCOL

=== PAGE 15 ===
CASCADE ACTIVATION WORKFLOW
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
STEP 1: Initial P90 Activation (2-11 AM EST)
  → Wait for P90 candle close >= threshold
  → Activate 40% size | Boundary: 80% of P90 body
  → Set direction of constraint resolution [3]: LONG or SHORT
  → Start 120-min resolution timer
STEP 2: 45-Min Add Check (+45 min from Signal 1)
  → Check if resolution output extended +8 pips
  → If YES: Activate 30% size | Boundary: Breakeven (Signal 1 output)
  → If NO: Skip, wait for cascade signal
STEP 3: Cascade P90 Check (30-90 min from Signal 1)
  → Watch for new P90 candle in SAME direction of constraint resolution
  → If SAME: Activate 20% size | Boundary: 168% of THIS P90 body (Stall Zone [10])
  → If OPPOSITE: IGNORE (opposite direction = noise, not reversal)
STEP 4: Cascade 2 P90 Check (60-90 min from Signal 1)
  → If SAME direction: Activate 10% size | Boundary: 168% of THIS P90 body
  → STOP here (max 3 cascades — resolution momentum exhausts at 4th+)
STEP 5: Exit Management
  → TP1 (-25%): Close 50% of total, move boundary to Breakeven
  → TP2 (-50%): Close remaining
  → Hard Exit (12 PM EST): Close ALL (constraint resolution window ends)
  → Kill Switch (132% State [10]): Close ALL immediately

End of Part 5 | P90P Enhanced Formula v2.0 | CEREBUS FX v18.2.23 | March 2026

KEY FINDINGS — FLOAT RATE BY TIER:
  Tier 1 (<20p):  71.1% chance Tuesday Asian never tests Monday range
                  33.3% full-day float (strong for small constraint deficits)
  Tier 2 (20-30p): 54.5% Tue Asian float | 37.9% full-day float ← HIGHEST full-day rate
  Tier 3 (30-45p): 31.1% Tue float | 13.3% full-day float — too rare to trade
  BIAS NOTE: Weekly directional bias (bull vs bear) has almost NO effect on float rate.
  Float is a structural phenomenon driven by constraint deficit size, not direction.
TEST 2: RETEST TIMING — FLOAT CONTEXT
IMPORTANT CONTEXT ON RETEST TIMING:
  Monday 08:00 UTC = London Open → Price frequently tests the Asian band immediately.
  This is normal London session behavior — not the float signal.
  The TRUE float signal is whether the SUBSEQUENT Asian session (Tuesday 00:00-08:00 UTC)
  tests the Monday band. That requires price to have stayed outside through:
    → Mon London session  → Mon NY session  → Mon close  → Tue Asian open
  That is a genuine ~17-20h sustained directional move — a weekly constraint float.
  Tier 1-2 Float Rates:
    T1: 71.1% of Tue Asian sessions never test Monday range
    T2: 54.5% — both confirm the float as a real weekly structural phenomenon

KEY FINDING — TIMING:
  Counter-trend extreme (pullback in bull weeks, bounce in bear) forms at median 26-27h.
  Calendar: Mon Asian closes 08:00 UTC + 27h = Tue 11:00 UTC → Tuesday mid-morning European.
  Mon + Tue combined = 65-66% of all counter-trend extremes already formed.
  The "mid-week shift" in the charts is the CONTINUATION off that Mon/Tue extreme.
  Weekly extreme (full high/low): median ~58h → Thu Asian / early London.
  This is when the field completes its weekly constraint deficit resolution.

KEY FINDING — FLOAT WEEK STRUCTURE:
  In TRUE float weeks (Tue full day outside Mon range):
  63.0% of counter-trend extremes ALREADY FORMED ON MONDAY.
  The market made its pullback/bounce during Mon London/NY session — before Tue opened.
  Then price resumes the directional move with no Tue Asian retest.
  The mid-week test in the charts = price returning to Mon range after 48h+ of float.
  FLOAT vs NON-FLOAT timing difference:
    Float weeks:     counter-trend extreme at 7h median (Monday itself)
    Non-float weeks: counter-trend extreme at 30h median (Tue morning/afternoon)
  This bimodal behavior IS the float mechanism — float weeks exhaust their counter-trend
  on Monday, then run cleanly for 48-72h toward 161-261% weekly targets.

WEEKLY FLOAT OPERATIONAL FRAMEWORK
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
MONDAY PRE-SESSION (1:45 AM EST):
  1. Measure Monday Asian Range → Classify Tier (T1/T2/T3/T4)
  2. Mark Monday Asian HIGH and LOW as WEEKLY STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINT BOUNDARIES
  3. Float probability: T1=71% | T2=55% | T3=31% | T4=skip
  4. Mark fib extension states from Mon Asian range:
       Bull targets: 100%, 162%, 168%, 200%, 261% above Mon Asian High
       Bear targets: -68%, -100%, -161% below Mon Asian Low
MONDAY SESSION WATCH (3 AM – 12 PM EST):
  London open breaks out of Asian constraint band.
  Watch: does the counter-trend partial rebalancing happen on Monday?
    → 43-44% of counter-trend extremes form ON MONDAY itself
    → In float weeks: 63% form within first 12h (Mon session)
    → The low/high that forms on Monday = key constraint support/resistance all week
FLOAT CONFIRMATION (Tuesday Asian 00:00-08:00 UTC):
  If Tuesday Asian session does NOT overlap Monday Asian band → FLOAT CONFIRMED
  → Occurs 52.6% of Tier 1-2 weeks
  → Hold directional bias | Target 161-261% fib states
  → Expected weekly range: 5-9x Monday Asian range (tier dependent)
MID-WEEK RETEST EXPECTATION:
  Weekly constraint deficit fully resolves at median ~58h from Mon Asian close
  → Thursday Asian / early London
  → The mid-week charts show price returning to Mon range from outside
  → Either: bounces back (continuation, more common on 48h float)
  → Or:     breaks back through (less common, T3 weeks mostly)

KEY FINDING — PARTIAL REBALANCING DISTRIBUTION:
  64.4% of days = FULL reversal (>100% of Asian range rebalanced).
  The dominant daily behavior is a full constraint re-resolution, not a float.

  TIER CONSISTENCY: ~18.5-20.3% float rate across all tiers — remarkably uniform.
  1 in 5 days holds outside the constraint band all day regardless of range size.

CONSTRAINT BOUNDARY PLACEMENT — FLOAT DAY DATA:
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
  Median partial rebalancing from extension peak on float days = 14.4 pips

  Maximum ever on a float day                                  = 65.7 pips

  18% of float days come within 3 pips of band before continuing
  DERIVED CONSTRAINT BOUNDARY RULE:
  → For 90% confidence on float days: boundary must absorb up to 36.8p from peak
  → In practice: Boundary AT or slightly inside the Asian High/Low covers this
  → Price that M5-CLOSES back inside the constraint band has 81.2% chance of NOT floating
  → Exit on M5 close back inside band — do not wait

TEST 6: CONSTRAINT BOUNDARY PLACEMENT FRAMEWORK
CONSTRAINT BOUNDARY PLACEMENT TIERS — DAILY FLOAT / RUN-AND-RETEST TRADES
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
TIER A — TIGHT BOUNDARY (Shallow float confirmed, 23.6-38.2% partial rebalancing stall):
  Condition: Partial rebalancing stalls at 38.2% or 23.6% of initial extension leg
  Boundary placement: AT Asian High (bull) or Asian Low (bear) — 0-2p buffer
  Logic: 38.2% stall holds outside band with avg 0.8p precision.
         Any M5 candle CLOSE back inside band = 81% chance of full reversal → exit.
  Typical boundary distance from activation: initial_ext + 2p (usually 8-18p)
TIER B — STANDARD BOUNDARY (Moderate stall, 50-61.8% of initial leg):
  Condition: Partial rebalancing to 50% or 61.8% of leg, still above constraint band
  Boundary placement: 5-8p below Asian High (bull) / above Asian Low (bear)
  Logic: Median buffer on float days = 8.4p. Covers 82% of float days.
  Breakeven trigger: move to breakeven when continuation reaches 20p from band
TIER C — WIDE BOUNDARY (Deep stall, 78.6% of leg, approaching constraint band):
  Condition: Partial rebalancing reaches 78.6% of initial extension leg
  Boundary placement: AT Asian High/Low exactly (0p buffer — exit on M5 close inside)
  Logic: 78.6% stall holds with 1.0p avg. Close inside band = invalidation.
INVALIDATION RULE (all tiers):
  M5 CANDLE CLOSE back INSIDE the Asian constraint band = EXIT immediately.
  81.2% of band re-entries continue through the full constraint range.
  This is the structural invalidation signal, not a wick touch.

KEY INSIGHT — REGIME ACCURACY vs CLOSURE PROBABILITY:
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
  REGIME ACCURACY (97% for CONFIRMED):
    = "Did price move in the predicted direction of constraint resolution?"
    = VolBand >1.2 predicted expansion → did price expand beyond CRD?
    = Binary outcome: YES / NO
  CLOSURE PROBABILITY (84.1% for CONFIRMED):
    = "Did price close within the PRECISION ZONE of the exact target?"
    = Target was 72.5 pips ±3.0 pips → did close land in [69.5, 75.5]?
    = Continuous outcome: distance from target
  WHY THE GAP?
  Even when the regime direction is correct (97%), the EXACT target has variance:
    • Fib stall zones have 0.5-1.5p natural variance (per daily float data)
    • Spread/slippage/news spikes add noise
    • The 97% says "direction right" — the 84.1% says "target precise"
  PRACTICAL USE:
    Use Regime Accuracy for POSITION SIZE (CONFIRMED = full size)
    Use Closure Probability for TARGET ADJUSTMENT (84.1% = expect ±3p variance)

SECTION 5: OPERATIONAL RULES DERIVED FROM BACKTEST
Entry Confirmation — 12 PM EST Checkpoint

=== PAGE 56 ===
REGIME CLASSIFICATION LOGIC (12 PM EST)
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
  1. Classify Tier from Asian Range (as per manual tiers T1/T2/T3/SKIP)
  2. Calculate Current Range Delta (CRD):
     CRD = High(7PM → now) - Low(7PM → now)
  3. Calculate Volatility Band:
     VolBand = Tier Average Daily Range / CRD
  4. Classify Regime:
     VolBand > 1.2  → CONFIRMED   (97% direction accuracy — full size)
     VolBand 0.8-1.2 → NORMAL     (79% direction accuracy — standard size)
     VolBand < 0.8  → COMPRESSION (67% direction accuracy — reduce size)
  5. Calculate Closure Probability (weighted function):
     Tier weight:    35%  (T1=95pts, T2=90pts, T3=85pts)
     VolBand weight: 30%  (scaled 70-95 based on VolBand value)
     Time weight:    20%  (scaled to completion factor)
     Regime weight:  10%  (CONFIRMED=90, NORMAL=85, COMPRESSION=80)
     1hr Vol weight:  5%  (avg 1-hr range proxy for noise)
Constraint Boundary Placement — Per Backtest Validation

SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE The Dual-Engine model splits capital deployment across two complementary
constraint-resolution engines. The Constraint Anchor captures the high-probability structural expansion of the
Asian constraint deficit [11] with wide structural boundaries [8] and high win rate. Resolution Amplifiers exploit
the momentum path through P90 activation signals [9], generating higher R-multiples when aligned with the
Anchor's direction of constraint resolution [3]. The critical finding: Amplifiers aligned with the Anchor
outperform standalone P90 trades by +15.3% win rate and +0.86R per activation.
SECTION 1: CONSTRAINT ANCHOR PERFORMANCE — THE CERTAINTY LAYER
Constraint Anchor: A structural activation [9] at the Asian constraint band violation [5]. The Anchor captures the full constraint deficit
expansion [11] to the -50% distribution target. Constraint Boundary [8] is placed at the OPPOSITE Asian extreme — structural
invalidation if resolution output [2] travels through the entire range.
Anchor Definition — The Certainty Layer Algorithm

=== PAGE 59 ===
CONSTRAINT ANCHOR DEFINITION
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
  PREREQUISITE: Asian Range < 30 pips (T1 or T2 ONLY)
    → Edge degrades sharply in wider constraint deficits [11]
    → Anchor only fires when the coiled spring is tight
  TIME WINDOW: 08:00-17:00 UTC (3:00 AM - 12:00 PM EST)
    → Covers London Open (primary constraint delivery) + early NY session
    → Avoids low-volume Asian session and late NY chop
  ACTIVATION SIGNAL [9]:
    → M5 candle must CLOSE outside Asian High or Low
    → Body >= 4.6 pips (P90 threshold — filters failed constraint violations)
    → LONG Anchor: Close > Asian High AND High > Asian High
    → SHORT Anchor: Close < Asian Low AND Low < Asian Low
  CONSTRAINT BOUNDARY [8]:
    → LONG: Opposite extreme = Asian Low (structural invalidation)
    → SHORT: Opposite extreme = Asian High (structural invalidation)
    → If resolution output crosses opposite extreme, the constraint set is fully invalidated
  TARGETS (Constraint Deficit Completion Zones):
    → TP1: Asian Range × 0.25 — 25% extension | Hit rate ~89-93% in T1/T2
    → TP2: Asian Range × 0.50 — 50% extension | Hit rate ~83% in T1/T2

CORE INSIGHT — MODEL 2 ACROSS ALL TIERS:
  Model 2 is a HIGH-PROBABILITY system across all tiers. Even in T3, 76.7% win rate

  T3 is the "Defensive" (77%, +2.1R) — conservative size, strict discipline required.

SECTION 9: EXIT LOGIC OPTIMIZATION — SEPARATED BY ENGINE
The test confirmed that treating Constraint Anchor and Resolution Amplifier exits identically is suboptimal. Each engine has a
different constraint resolution completion profile.
Engine
Exit 1
Exit 2
Trailing Logic
Constraint Anchor
Close 50% at -25% extension (91% hit
rate)
Close remaining at -50% extension (80%
hit rate)
Move ALL boundaries to
BE+2p when TP1 hit
Resolution Amplifier
Close 100% at fixed 20p OR 1:2.5 RR
No extended holding — scalp the path
Exit fully when target hit —
no runners
EXIT LOGIC REASONING:
  CONSTRAINT ANCHOR exits:
    Holding to -100% extension drops win rate to ~60% without adding significant R.
    Best close at -25% (TP1: 91% hit rate) + -50% (TP2: 80% hit rate).
    At TP1, move ALL boundaries (Anchor + Amplifiers) to Breakeven + 2 pips.
    This locks the certainty-layer profit while allowing Amplifier path scalps.
  RESOLUTION AMPLIFIER exits:
    Amplifiers are path-exploitation activations [9] — designed as quick scalps.
    Holding for large extensions increases variance without adding meaningful R.
    Fixed 20-pip target or 1:2.5 RR is optimal. Exit immediately when hit.
    Do NOT hold Amplifiers to Anchor distribution targets (-50%).
  THE HIERARCHY:
    Anchor = distribution ride (structural) → exit at completion zones
    Amplifier = path exploitation (tactical) → exit at fixed scalp targets

DUAL-ENGINE DEPLOYMENT PROTOCOL
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
STEP 1: PRE-SESSION SCAN (1:45-3:00 AM EST)
  I Measure Asian Range (constraint deficit [11])
  I Classify Tier (T1/T2/T3/SKIP)
  I Note early momentum — check overfilled status at 9 AM
STEP 2: CONSTRAINT ANCHOR DEPLOYMENT
  I Trigger: Constraint violation close [5] outside Asian band + Body >= 4.6p (P90 threshold)
  I Size:
      T1: 60% of Max Risk  |  T2: 70% of Max Risk  |  T3: 100% of Max Risk
  I Constraint Boundary [8]: Opposite Asian extreme (structural invalidation)
  I Target: TP1 = -25% extension (50% position) | TP2 = -50% extension (50% position)
  I At TP1: Move ALL boundaries (Anchor + Amps) to Breakeven + 2 pips
STEP 3: RESOLUTION AMPLIFIER DEPLOYMENT (Conditional — T1/T2 Only)
  I Condition: ONLY if Tier T1 or T2 AND direction matches Anchor [3]
  I Trigger: P90 activation signal [9] pulling back to partial rebalancing zone [6]
      T1: 32% or 50% partial rebalancing (max 2 Amplifiers — each 20% size)
      T2: 50% partial rebalancing ONLY (max 1 Amplifier — 30% size)
      T3: NO AMPLIFIERS under any circumstances
  I Constraint Boundary: 80% Fib of P90 body
  I Target: Fixed 20 pips OR 1:2.5 RR — exit fully when hit
STEP 4: MANAGEMENT & EXIT
  I At Anchor TP1 (-25%): Close 50% Anchor | Move ALL boundaries to BE+2p
  I At Amplifier target: Close 100% of Amplifier immediately
  I At Anchor TP2 (-50%): Close remaining Anchor
  I Hard Exit: 12:00 PM EST — constraint resolution window closes
  I Kill Switch: 132% Kill-Switch State [10] → Close ALL immediately
STEP 5: OVERFILLED CHECK (9:00 AM EST)
  I If Daily Range > 40 pips by 9 AM:
      T1: Anchor-only, reduce to 50% size
      T2/T3: STAND DOWN — exit any open positions, no new activations
MACHINE LOGIC SUMMARY:
  1. Asian Range < 30p? → YES: Deploy Anchor | NO (T3): Model 2 only
  2. Constraint violation with P90 body >= 4.6p? → YES: Anchor activated
  3. P90 direction matches Anchor? → YES + T1: 2 Amps | YES + T2: 1 Amp | NO: 0 Amps
  4. At TP1: Lock Breakeven on all. At TP2: Close Anchor. Hard exit 12PM.

PART 11 FAILURE SEQUENCE ANALYSIS — THE REPAIR MODEL
Constraint Violation Failure Taxonomy | Day-of-Week Edge | First vs Second
Acceptance | Fail Box & Flip Timing EUR/USD M5 | 2022-2026 | 465 Valid
2h-Hold Setups | CEREBUS FX v18.2.40
Asian Break → 2-Hour Hold Model | Full Failure Sequence Study | Sep 2022 – Apr 2026 | Live Data
WHAT THIS PART ADDRESSES When the Asian constraint violation [5] holds for 2 hours but then fails before
reaching the 1x constraint deficit resolution target — what happens next? This study maps the full post-failure
resolution sequence: which structural levels the field [1] repairs to first, whether a second acceptance attempt
forms and how often it succeeds, and the precise timing and location of the failure itself. The core finding: failure
is not the end of the day. It is the beginning of a new resolution state.
SECTION 1: FAILURE SEQUENCE — CORE DATA
Failure Definition: After a valid 2-hour acceptance hold [5], failure = first M5 close back INSIDE the Asian constraint band before the 1x
constraint deficit resolution target [11] is hit. This is structural failure — not noise or a wick touch.

Post-Failure Level Sequence — What Gets Hit Most Often
Constraint band midpoint
73.8% of failures
Opposite constraint band edge
51.0% of failures
Opposite 1x resolution target
20.0% of failures

=== PAGE 70 ===
POST-FAILURE SEQUENCE — DOMINANT PATTERN:
  1. Valid 2h acceptance hold → price fails (M5 close back inside band)
  2. Constraint band midpoint hit first (73.8% of all failures)
  3. ~Half of those continue to opposite band edge (51.0% overall)
  4. Minority fully flip to opposite 1x target (20.0% overall)
  INTERPRETATION:
  The most common failure sequence is NOT "fail → full reversal."
  It is: fail → midpoint repair → new acceptance attempt → decide.
  The midpoint functions as the first structural rebalancing node [6]
  after a failed constraint violation [5] — analogous to how the
  168% Stall Zone [10] operates in the P90 model:
  P90 model:        168% = "extension too far → partial rebalancing first"
  Asian break model: midpoint = "acceptance failed → internal rebalancing first"
  Same role. First repair zone. Not final reversal proof.
SECTION 2: FAILURE TAXONOMY — THREE RESOLUTION STATES
Failure Type
What Happens
Frequency
Action
Type 1 — Soft Failure
(Midpoint repair only)
Fails back inside band → midpoint hit → field
compresses. No clean follow-through in either
direction.
Most common
Stand down. Wait for new close
signal.
Type 2 — Internal Reset
(Same-side recycle)
Fails → midpoint repair → field later reclaims
original side → second 2h acceptance →
constraint resolution continues.
Second most common
(89% of second
breaks)
Wait for second acceptance
hold. Re-enter aligned.
Type 3 — Regime Flip
(Opposite-side confirmed)
Fails → midpoint → opposite edge → opposite
constraint violation → second 2h hold → opposite
1x target prints.
Minority (11% of
second breaks)
Rarer but highest win rate
(84.6%). Wait for full
confirmation.
THE REAL POST-FAILURE STRUCTURE:
  Do NOT assume full direction of constraint resolution reversal [3] on failure.
  The dominant next event is midpoint repair — not opposite-side completion.
  Think of it as three sequential questions:
    1. Does price stall and compress at midpoint?   → Type 1 (stand down)
    2. Does price reclaim the original side?         → Type 2 (second entry same side)
    3. Does price drive through to opposite edge?    → Type 3 (regime flip confirmed)
  Only answer question 3 AFTER 2 and only trade it AFTER a new 2h acceptance hold.
  Emotional reaction to failure = worst execution decision.

  Playing it as a breakout day (Tue/Wed style) destroys edge systematically.

FLIP EXECUTION SEQUENCE — THE MONEY PATTERN:
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII

    Expect midpoint (73.8% probability) as first repair level [6]

    a) Does price stall and compress at midpoint? → Type 1 (stand down)
    b) Does price reclaim the original side? → Type 2 (same-side re-entry)
    c) Does price drive to opposite edge? → Type 3 (regime flip incoming)
  STEP 5 — ONLY take the next trade if:
    → A new M5 close signal fires outside the band (either side)
    → Price holds outside for 2 hours (full acceptance confirmation)
    → That second 2h hold = the real activation signal [9]
  THURSDAY SPECIALIST RULE:
  On Thursday, if the first constraint violation fails quickly (<30 min):
    → 70-80% probability the opposite side is the real resolution direction [3]
    → Load the opposite-side second accepted move when it confirms
    → Win rate on confirmed opposite-side Thursday flip: ~84% range
  THE MACHINE QUESTION:
  "Is today a FIRST-MOVE day… or a FLIP day?"
  → Tue/Wed: First move. Go hard on the initial violation.
  → Thu: Flip day. Wait. The second move is the trade.

THE QUARTERLY CLUSTER — WHAT IT LOOKS LIKE IN REAL TRADING:
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
  Because the win rate is high (~86-87%), losses tend to cluster rather
  than distribute evenly. The typical pattern:
    • 3 weeks of normal trading: only 1 losing day per week
    • Then 1 bad week: 4 losing days in a row — the "noise cluster"
    • That one bad week = the entire quarterly 4-day streak expectation
  The 4-day streak is not a "once in a blue moon" event.
  It is a quarterly occurrence — roughly 3-4 times per year.
  PRACTICAL FRAMEWORK FOR WHEN IT HITS:
    Days 1-2 loss: Normal noise. Execute next setup as usual.
    Day 3 loss:    The quarterly cluster is arriving. Stay the course.
    Day 4 loss:    Expected. Recovery begins mathematically within 11 days.
    Day 5+ loss:   Rare event. Check three filters:
                     → Is a Tier 4 (>45p) setup sneaking through?
                     → Major news disruption in the week?
                     → Regime FAILED status dominant?
                   If all three are clean: CONTINUE. Edge is intact.
                   Do not reduce size. Do not add extra filters.
                   The constraint-resolution edge [11] has not changed.
  The correct mental frame:
  "Three to four times this year, I will wake up on Day 3 of a losing streak.
   This is the quarterly tax for holding an 87% win rate system.
   When it happens, the correct action is to execute the next qualified setup."

TEST METHODOLOGY The Blind Structural Chain Test stripped away all time-based assumptions and tracked
only the event-driven constraint resolution sequence: Impulse (A) → Partial Rebalancing (B) → Continuation (C).
Time windows were NOT imposed — they were discovered after the fact from the data. The test ran across 1,008
trading days (Sep 2022 – Apr 2026), all valid T1/T2/T3 days. The core question: is the Impulse → Partial
Rebalancing → Continuation chain a structural law, or is it a random pattern? The answer defines how the
constraint resolution engine [11] actually operates.
SECTION 1: EXISTENCE PROOF — DOES THE CHAIN HAPPEN?

directional resolution output [2] breaking the Asian band. Partial Rebalancing (B) = temporary rebalancing [6] after the impulse.
Continuation (C) = the resumption of the direction of constraint resolution [3] to complete the deficit.

THE 80% CLOSE — CONSTRAINT BOUNDARY INVALIDATION [8]:

  cascade plan immediately. The chain is invalidated.
  Do NOT confuse a wick touch with a candle close — only the close counts.

THE BLIND CHAIN LAW — COMPLETE FORMULA:
  SUCCESS = (Impulse > Tier Threshold)
            AND (Partial Rebalancing in 32-50% zone) [6]
            AND (No M5 close past 80%) [8]
  1. WHEN price moves the Tier Threshold from 3 AM (T1: 10-12p, T2: 14-16p, T3: 18-20p)
  2. THEN watch for pullback to 32-50% of that impulse leg
  3. IF no M5 candle closes past 80% of the impulse
  4. THEN continuation is 93.7% probable within the next 40-92 minutes

    Partial rebalancing > 62%   → STAND DOWN (Trap Zone — 66% failure rate)
    M5 close past 80%           → INVALIDATED (Hard Kill — 93% failure rate)

  T2 (20-30p Asian): 14-16 pips

  NO-GO (>45p):      No valid threshold — negative expectancy
  These thresholds apply to EVERY cycle (Cycle 1, 2, and 3).
  For Cycle 2/3, if Regime is CONFIRMED (>1.5x ratio), the threshold can
  be reduced by 2 pips because momentum is already established.
SECTION 6: THE RECURSIVE LOOP ENGINE — TIER-BY-TIER
The constraint resolution chain does not operate as a single event. When the initial cycle fails to reach the daily distribution target
by 12 PM, the field [1] resets and runs the same sequence again with new variance parameters. This is the Recursive Loop Engine
— the mechanism behind what most traders misidentify as 'chop' or 'accumulation.'
Chop / Accumulation redefined: an incomplete constraint resolution cycle [11] that failed to reach the distribution target. It is not random
noise — it is Cycle N resetting before Cycle N+1 fires. The time window (2AM-12PM) is not a magic schedule; it is the institutional
volume window that provides the activation energy for each cycle. By 12PM, that volume dries up and the loop mechanism shuts down.

      NO  → Engine resets:
              New Impulse (A_n+1) forms from end of C_n

  The field uses previous cycles to clear available resolution pathways [4],
  making each subsequent cycle faster and shallower until the distribution
  target is satisfied. This is a staircase resolution — not an elevator.

THE 12PM HARD EXIT — STRUCTURAL EXPLANATION:
  The constraint resolution engine operates on institutional volume.
  The London/NY overlap (approximately 8AM-12PM EST) provides the activation
  energy required for each cycle's impulse phase.
  By 12PM EST, that volume dries up. Without the volume to form a valid Impulse (A),
  the loop mechanism cannot restart. Any cycle attempting to start after 12PM has
  less than 15% success rate and typically results in a fade or reversal into close.
  This is why the 12PM Hard Exit is not an arbitrary rule — it is the
  natural shutdown of the loop engine. By 12PM, 98.9% of the daily
  constraint deficit [11] has been resolved. The remaining 1.1% is noise.
  Post-12PM activity breakdown by tier:
    T1: Avg 0.01 cycles — effectively zero additional constraint resolution
    T2: Avg 0.04 cycles — trace activity, no tradeable impulse
    T3: Avg 0.12 cycles — occasional small move but insufficient for a real entry

SECTION 2: THE RECURSIVE SHIFT ENGINE — DECODING THE CHOP
Chop / Noise redefined at the shift level: When an impulse fails (M5 close returns >80% into the impulse), the field [1] does not reverse
randomly. It encodes a proportional counter-move of approximately 1.44x the failed impulse. This is the Recursive Shift Mechanism —
the algorithm that builds range via repeated proportional steps rather than a single directional burst.
The Dual-Path & Proportional Shift Analysis tracked every impulse failure across the full 4-year dataset (1,008 days). A failure was
defined as an impulse that closed back >80% into its own body without reclaiming the extreme within 5 bars (25 minutes). 418 shift
events detected.

          T1: 10-12p | T2: 14-16p | T3: 18-20p

    NO  → Continuation likely. Maintain position.

  T1: Requires 3-4 iterations of the loop (I → Shift → I → Shift) to reach target
  T2: 1-2 iterations
  T3: The first I is large enough that 1.44×I (or direct continuation) hits target
      The loop is rarely needed — one burst resolves the deficit
Trading the Shift — Operational Rules
SHIFT TRADE EXECUTION PROTOCOL:
  1. Identify failed impulse: move > Tier threshold that closes back >80%
  2. Confirm stall: price does NOT reclaim the high/low within 5 bars (25 min)
  3. Enter in the opposite direction of constraint resolution [3]
  4. Target: 1.44x the size of the failed impulse (TP)
     Example: Failed bull impulse = 12 pips → Bear shift target = 12 × 1.44 = 17.3 pips
  5. Stop: Above the failed impulse high (or below low for bull shift)
CONTEXT FILTERS:
  2+ shifts already visible today → expect T1/Staircase behavior
                                   → keep trading the loops
  0 shifts + large single impulse → expect T3/Monolith behavior
                                   → do NOT fade it — ride continuation [3]
SECTION 3: TRIGGER-OPPOSE EXTREME SL — PRECISION CONSTRAINT
BOUNDARY

=== PAGE 108 ===
Trigger-Oppose Pair: The two-candle structural unit that defines a shift. The Trigger Candle (C_trigger) is the last strong candle
completing the impulse. The Opposite Candle (C_opp) is the very next candle that closes AGAINST the impulse. The extreme of this
two-candle pair — the combined high (for a short shift) or combined low (for a long shift) — is the structural constraint boundary [8].
Breaking this level means the shift has failed and the original impulse is resuming.
The Trigger-Oppose SL protocol replaces waiting for a deep retrace entry with an immediate structural entry. Instead of waiting for
the 32-50% rebalancing zone to form over 30-78 minutes, the entry fires on the close of the Opposite Candle with the two-candle
extreme as the hard constraint boundary [8]. This compresses the setup from a 30-78 minute window to a single 5-minute bar.
Setup Logic — Step by Step
TRIGGER-OPPOSE EXTREME SL — STRUCTURAL LOGIC:
  1. IMPULSE LEG: Price moves > Tier threshold (T1: 10-12p, T2: 14-16p, T3: 18-20p)
  2. TRIGGER CANDLE (C_trigger): The last strong candle completing or extending the impulse
  3. OPPOSITE CANDLE (C_opp): The very next candle that closes AGAINST the impulse
  4. ENTRY: Market entry on the CLOSE of C_opp (no waiting for retrace)
  5. CONSTRAINT BOUNDARY [8]: Extreme high/low of the combined C_trigger + C_opp pair
       For a SHORT shift: SL = MAX(C_trigger high, C_opp high)
       For a LONG shift:  SL = MIN(C_trigger low, C_opp low)
  6. TARGET: 1.44x the measured impulse size (Shift Rule from Section 2)
  INVALIDATION LOGIC:
    If price breaks the combined extreme of the two-candle pair, the shift has
    failed — the original direction of constraint resolution [3] is resuming.
    Exit immediately. Do not wait for further confirmation.

Why the Two-Candle Extreme Is a Structural Pivot
STRUCTURAL PIVOT LOGIC:
  The combined extreme of C_trigger + C_opp marks the boundary where
  the impulse direction of constraint resolution [3] and the shift
  direction of constraint resolution meet.
  If price breaks this level, it confirms that:
    a) The original impulse momentum is still active
    b) C_opp was a temporary partial rebalancing [6], not a shift initiation
    c) The constraint deficit [11] is still resolving in the original direction
  The two-candle structure absorbs the natural M5 volatility (wicks above/below)
  while defining the exact point where the shift thesis is invalidated.
  This is more precise than an arbitrary pip buffer because it uses the
  market's own structure — the actual printed extremes — as the boundary.
  STALL CONFIRMATION (5-bar rule):
  For added conviction, confirm the shift by checking that price does NOT
  reclaim the impulse extreme within 5 bars (25 minutes) of C_opp close.
  This eliminates fast false signals where the "opposite" candle was a wick,
  not a genuine shift.
Trigger-Oppose Protocol — Execution Checklist

=== PAGE 110 ===
TRIGGER-OPPOSE EXTREME SL — LIVE EXECUTION CHECKLIST:
  I IMPULSE DETECTED:
     Price has moved > Tier threshold from 3AM base (or previous cycle end)
     T1: ≥10p | T2: ≥14p | T3: ≥18p
  I TRIGGER CANDLE IDENTIFIED:
     The last strong directional M5 candle completing or extending the impulse
     Note its extreme: High (bull trigger) or Low (bear trigger)
  I OPPOSITE CANDLE PRINTS:
     Next M5 candle closes in the OPPOSITE direction to the impulse
     Entry fires on the CLOSE of this candle — no waiting required
  I SL CALCULATED:
     SHORT shift: SL = MAX(C_trigger high, C_opp high) + 0.5p buffer
     LONG shift:  SL = MIN(C_trigger low, C_opp low) - 0.5p buffer
     Check risk: If SL > 15p from entry → skip (setup too wide)
  I TARGET CALCULATED:
     Target = Entry ± (Impulse Size × 1.44)
     Secondary: 1.20x minimum | 1.68x maximum (regime flip territory above this)
  I STALL CONFIRMATION (OPTIONAL, HIGHER CONVICTION):
     Does price fail to reclaim the impulse extreme within 5 bars (25 min)?
     YES → Shift confirmed, hold the entry
     NO  → Caution — impulse may be resuming
  I CONTEXT CHECK:
     Multiple shifts already today → T1/Staircase — continue trading loops
     No shifts + large impulse     → T3/Monolith — do NOT fade, ride continuation

SETUP 2 — TEMPORAL DELIVERY + EWS EXIT: LIVE FRAMEWORK
  AT ACTIVATION (first M5 close outside Asian band):
    Note the time → Start the tier delivery clock
    T1: expect -25% by 1.5h, -50% by 2.8h
    T2: expect -25% by 2.2h, -50% by 4.1h
  WHEN -25% IS HIT:
    Fast delivery (within tier median):  Hold standard. Monitor for EWS.
    Slow delivery (>2× tier median):     Close full position at -25%. Done.
    EWS prints at -25% (opposite P90 ≥4.6p close): Close remaining 50% immediately.
  BETWEEN -25% AND -50%:
    This is the partial rebalancing zone [6] — where variance lives.
    If time elapsed > 1.5× tier median and price still short of -50%:
      → Trail boundary to BE+2p | Use EWS as the exit trigger
    If any M5 candle CLOSES back inside Asian constraint band:
      → Exit immediately (81.2% rule — field reversing constraint resolution [5])
  WHEN -50% IS HIT:
    Standard close: take full profits at -50%
    EWS prints at -50% (opposite P90 ≥4.6p): Close 100% — constraint deficit resolved
    Unicorn (T1, fast delivery, Regime CONFIRMED): small runner to 168% Stall Zone [10]
  TIMING KILL SWITCHES:
    T1 — -50% not hit by 4.2h from activation → use EWS as exit signal
    T2 — -50% not hit by 6.2h from activation → use EWS as exit signal
    10:00 AM EST — if -25% not yet hit → stand down, no new activations
    12:00 PM EST — hard exit, all positions closed regardless of target status

WHY THE SYNERGY GAP HOLDS HERE (Part 10):
  Aligned cascade P90s (same direction): 87.8% win rate
  Opposite cascade P90s (Cascade EWS):   64.8% win rate
  The 23% gap exists because the field [1] has a strong preference
  to continue the initial direction of constraint resolution [3].
  An opposite cascade is almost always a partial rebalancing pause [6],
  not a full regime flip. Only 18.3% of Cascade EWS events represent
  a genuine direction of constraint resolution reversal.
  Regime filter explains the 12.3% win rate jump:
  When Regime = CONFIRMED, the field is actively expanding — opposite
  cascades are near-certain noise (58.4% WR).
  When Regime = FAILED, the field is compressing — opposite cascades
  have genuine reversal potential (77.1% WR).
Conditional Reversal Entry — Full Requirements
CASCADE EWS REVERSAL ENTRY — ONLY VALID WHEN ALL FOUR CONDITIONS MET:
  1. Regime = FAILED at 9 AM (ratio <1.45x)
  2. Cascade EWS fires in 45-60 min window (opposite P90 ≥ threshold)
  3. Resolution output [2] CLOSES beyond 200% Deep State [10] of initial P90
  4. Price HOLDS outside original constraint band for 2 hours (Model 2 confirmation)
  When all four conditions are met: win rate = 77.1%
  If any condition is missing: reversal entry drops below 65% — not a CEREBUS trade
  This is the Model 2 two-hour hold protocol from Part 12 applied to intraday
  cascade context. The 2-hour hold is not optional — it is the confirmation
  that the direction of constraint resolution [3] has genuinely shifted.

SETUP 5 — 5-DAY ANCHOR MACRO SETUP — MONTHLY CONSTRAINT RESOLUTION
The macro setup uses the first five trading days of each month as the structural constraint anchor — the monthly equivalent of the
Asian Range. A daily close outside the 5-day High/Low combined with a 2-hour M5 hold produces an 81-84% win rate to the 2.0x
(200%) extension target by Trading Day 15. Validated across EUR/USD daily data 2022-2026.
THE 2-HOUR HOLD FILTER The same Model 2 confirmation protocol from intraday T3 (Part 12) applies here at
macro scale. An M5 close outside the 5-day band is not sufficient alone — price must HOLD outside for a full two
hours before the activation is considered valid. This removes approximately 35% of false breakouts and lifts
signal validity from ~50% to 65-78%. The 81.2% close-back-inside rule also applies: if an M5 candle closes back
inside the 5-day band at any point, exit immediately.

TIER LOGIC — WHY THE THRESHOLDS SCALE:
  M-T1 (less than 60p): Compressed first week = coiled spring = violent expansion.
    Fast resolution, often hits target by Day 9-10. Highest win rate (86%).

    Median hit Day 11-12. The core bread-and-butter macro tier.

    Slower grind, more false breaks. Use 1.6x target (not 2.0x).
    Win rate 75% — trade it smaller and take profits earlier.
  NO-GO (over 125p): Monthly constraint deficit largely priced in.
    Edge collapses. Skip the month entirely.

Why This Works
THREE REASONS THE 5-DAY ANCHOR MACRO SETUP WORKS:
  1. THE 5-DAY ANCHOR:
     Captures the full weekly institutional positioning cycle.
     One week of price action contains the complete opening range before
     directional flows resolve the monthly constraint deficit [11].
     A 3-day range captures incomplete positioning; 5 days captures it fully.
  2. THE 2-HOUR HOLD FILTER:
     The same Model 2 confirmation protocol proven in intraday T3 (Part 12)
     operates identically at macro scale. Any breakout that fails the 2h hold
     was a false constraint violation [5]. The filter removes 35% of fakes
     and lifts signal validity from ~50% to 65-78%.
  3. THE 2.0x TARGET:
     The 2.0x (200%) extension is the Deep State stall zone [10] at macro scale.
     This is where the monthly constraint deficit [11] fully resolves.
     You are not chasing price — you are waiting for the completion curve
     to arrive at its natural termination point. Average precision: ±1.5 pips.

SETUP 5 — 5-DAY ANCHOR MACRO: EXECUTION FRAMEWORK
  STEP 1: PRE-SETUP (Day 5 Close)
    Measure: High and Low of Trading Days 1-5
    Calculate 5-day range (High - Low)
    Classify Tier: below 60p = M-T1 | 60-90p = M-T2 | 90-125p = M-T3 | above 125p = skip
    Mark: 5-Day High and 5-Day Low on chart (the monthly anchor band)
    Mark: 2.0x extension above High (bull) and below Low (bear)
    Mark: Day 15 on calendar (hard exit)
  STEP 2: ACTIVATION TRIGGER (Days 6-15)
    Wait for an M5 candle to CLOSE outside the 5-Day High or Low
    Direction: LONG if close above 5-Day High | SHORT if close below 5-Day Low
    Start 2-hour timer immediately on the M5 close
  STEP 3: 2-HOUR HOLD CONFIRMATION
    If price stays outside the 5-day band for 2 full hours: VALID ACTIVATION
    If any M5 candle closes back inside the band during 2h: SKIP THIS SIGNAL
    Entry option A: Enter on the M5 close that confirms the 2h hold
    Entry option B: Wait for a shallow pullback (32-50% of initial leg) to enter
  STEP 4: STOP LOSS PLACEMENT
    Place stop AT the 5-Day band edge (5-Day High for shorts | 5-Day Low for longs)
    If any M5 candle closes back inside the 5-day band after entry: EXIT IMMEDIATELY
    No exceptions. Wicks don't count. Closes do.
  STEP 5: TARGET AND MANAGEMENT
    M-T1 and M-T2: Primary target = 2.0x extension of the 5-day range
      Close 70% of position at 2.0x
      Trail remaining 30% to 2.61x only if Regime CONFIRMED (ratio above 1.5x)
    M-T3: Primary target = 1.6x extension (do not push to 2.0x)
      Close 100% at 1.6x — wider range reduces continuation probability
  STEP 6: HARD EXIT
    Day 15 close — close ALL positions regardless of target status
    The monthly constraint resolution window closes at Day 15
    Holding beyond Day 15 fights a dead engine (sub-30% continuation rate)

  LONG:  M5 close below 5-Day Low  → EXIT NOW

  Only candle closes matter.
  No exceptions.

    81.2% of M5 closes back inside the band continue through the full range.

    The remaining 18.8% that recover are not worth the risk of holding.
    Wick touches the band: stay in.
    M5 candle body closes inside: exit. No discussion.

SETUP 6 — POST-FAILURE REPAIR SEQUENCE — MIDPOINT AS DECISION ZONE, NOT
REVERSAL
When a Setup 5 (5-Day Anchor) activation fails — price closes back inside the band and stops you out — what happens next is not
random. The failure sequence data from Part 11 (465 setups, 210 failures) maps exactly what the field [1] does after a failed
constraint violation [5]. This setup defines how to read the repair sequence and when — and when not — to re-enter.
CRITICAL: THE MIDPOINT IS NOT A REVERSAL SIGNAL 73.8% of failures hit the midpoint of the 5-day range
first. This is a structural repair node — the field rebalancing, not committing to a new direction. Of failures that
form a second move, 89% recycle back to the ORIGINAL breakout side (same-side re-acceptance). Only 11%
genuinely flip to the opposite band. Targeting the opposite band from the midpoint trades against an 89%
probability. Midpoint = Observe. Opposite Band + 2h Hold = Execute.
The Actual Post-Failure Sequence
Based on Part 11 failure sequence data, cross-applied to the macro 5-day anchor. Example: bullish breakout above 5-Day High is
stopped out.

WHY 89% RECYCLE SAME-SIDE (NOT FLIP):
  The midpoint absorbs the failed breakout liquidity. It is where available
  resolution pathways [4] are harvested before the field [1] attempts
  re-acceptance on the same side.
  Most failures are not regime flips — they are failed acceptance attempts.
  The field repairs the failed constraint violation [5], compresses at the
  midpoint, then re-attempts the original direction of constraint resolution [3].
  75-80% of macro failures: midpoint pause → resume original direction
  15-20% of macro failures: midpoint pause → opposite side flip
  ~5%: compression with no follow-through either direction
  Trading against 89% is not a strategy. It is prediction.

Midpoint Re-Entry — When the Same Side Resumes
When price returns to the midpoint and shows a confirmation close back in the original direction of constraint resolution [3], it
signals the repair phase [6] is complete and the original monthly bias is resuming. This is the secondary entry — not a reversal
trade, but a continuation of the original setup.
MIDPOINT RE-ENTRY SEQUENCE (Same-Side Continuation):
IIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII
  1. Initial breakout fails → M5 close back inside 5-day band → exit
  2. Price rebalances to the 50% midpoint of the 5-day range (73.8% frequency)
  3. TRIGGER — one of two confirmation signals at the midpoint:
       (a) M5 candle closes back in the ORIGINAL breakout direction
           (bull setup: green M5 close above midpoint)
           (bear setup: red M5 close below midpoint)
       (b) Rejection candle: wick into midpoint, closes back in original direction
  4. Entry: market order on the close of the confirmation candle
  5. Stop loss: 2-3 pips beyond the midpoint on the opposite side
     If price closes back inside the band after entry → exit immediately
  6. Targets (from original breakout edge, not from midpoint):
       TP1: Opposite band edge (65-70% hit rate) — quick scalp through failure zone
       TP2: 100% extension (50-55% hit rate) — standard continuation
       TP3/Runner: 168% extension (30-35%) — only if price holds outside band 2h post-TP1
  7. Size: 60-70% of original activation size (secondary entry, not primary)
  8. Rebalance duration: 1-3 days (macro moves grind, not snap)
     Confirmation is mandatory — do not enter on a wick touch alone

Opposite-Side Flip — Full Confirmation Required (11% of Failures)
OPPOSITE SIDE FLIP ENTRY — ALL CONDITIONS REQUIRED:
  Frequency: only 11% of failures produce a genuine opposite-side flip
  1. Initial breakout failed → stopped at band edge
  2. Price rebalanced to midpoint
  3. Price continued to the OPPOSITE band (5-Day Low for a bull failure)
  4. M5 candle CLOSES outside the opposite band
  5. Price HOLDS outside for 2 full hours (same 2h hold filter as Setup 5)
  6. ONLY then: enter in the new direction
  7. Win rate on confirmed flips: 84.6%
  NEVER enter the flip at the midpoint or before the opposite band is reached.
  The midpoint is where 89% of the field's traffic reverses back to the original side.
  The opposite band + 2h hold is the only valid flip confirmation.

THE CONVERGENCE FACTOR (PHI) — WIN RATE SCALING
Phi is the field state score from 0.0 to 1.0. It aggregates the four confirmation layers and scales the win rate deterministically. At Phi
= 1.0 (all conditions met), win rate reaches 98.7%. Below Phi 0.6 the setup is a NO-GO.
PHI = (0.40 × Regime) + (0.25 × P90) + (0.20 × Cascade) + (0.15 × Float)
  Regime:  1.0 if Ratio >= 1.50  |  0.7 if Ratio 1.45-1.49  |  0.5 if < 1.45
  P90:     1.0 if confirmed in 2-6 AM window
  Cascade: 1.0 if printed in 45-60 min optimal window
  Float:   1.0 if Monday/Tuesday Float confirmed
PHI TO WIN RATE:
  Phi = 1.0 (all conditions): P_Win = 98.7%
  Phi >= 0.8:                 P_Win = 91-94%  (High conviction)
  Phi >= 0.6:                 P_Win = 85%     (Base — minimum acceptable)
  Phi < 0.6:                  NO-GO           (Do not trade)

FIXED DOLLAR EXPECTANCY (FDE) — TARGET-FIRST SIZING
Stop sizing by fixed % risk. Size by fixed $ target. With 98.7% accuracy and EWS filtering, failure is rare and small. The only
variable is pip distance to the target. FDE normalizes the payout so every trade prints the same dollar amount regardless of
whether it is a tight T1 day or a wide T3 day.

=== PAGE 139 ===
LOT SIZE = Target Dollar Profit / (Atomic Target Pips x Pip Value)
  T1 Day (10p Atomic, $10/pip, target $50):  0.50 Lots
  T2 Day (14p Atomic, $10/pip, target $50):  0.36 Lots
  T3 Day (18p Atomic, $10/pip, target $50):  0.28 Lots
  Result: Every win pays $50 regardless of tier.
  Equity curve is a straight line up — zero lumpy returns.
WHY NOT FIXED % RISK:
  Fixed % risk on T1 (tight SL) vs T3 (wide SL) creates wildly different
  dollar outcomes per trade — destroying the equity curve's smoothness.
  FDE inverts this: the LARGER the pip distance, the FEWER the lots,
  so the dollar output stays constant at every tier.
THE CEREBUS GRAND UNIFIED EQUATION
Expected Return ($) =
  [ Target $ / (Atomic Target_Tier x Pip Value) ]     <- Dynamic Lot Size
  x
  [ (Atomic Target_Tier x P_Win(Phi))                 <- Weighted Expectancy
    - (SL_Structural x (1 - P_EWS)) ]                 <- Filtered Risk
VARIABLES:
  Atomic Target_Tier  = T1:10p | T2:12p | T3:15p
  P_Win(Phi)          = Win rate at current field state (87-98.7%)
  SL_Structural       = Structural constraint boundary [8] in pips
  P_EWS               = Probability EWS exits before SL is hit (~78%)
EXAMPLE (T2 Day, Phi=1.0, target $50):
  Lot Size = $50 / (14p x $10) = 0.357 lots
  Expected = 0.357 x [(14p x 0.987) - (tiny SL x 0.013)]
           = ~$49.35 per trade
The key insight: tighter SL + higher P_EWS shrinks the filtered risk term,
improving expected return without needing higher win rate.

    for session, day_data in df.groupby('session'):
        asian = day_data[(day_data.index.hour>=19)|(day_data.index.hour<3)]
        ar = asian['high'].max() - asian['low'].min()
        # Classify tier — NO-GO if AR > T3 max
        # LAYER 1: LOCK BIAS — first M5 close outside Asian band
        for idx, row in bias_window.iterrows():

        # LAYER 2: ATOMIC ENTRY — impulse (body >= atomic*0.5) + opposite close
        for idx, row in post_bias.iterrows():

                    entry = next_candle['close']; break  # LONG ENTRY

                if next_candle['close'] > next_candle['open']:
                    entry = next_candle['close']; break  # SHORT ENTRY
        # LAYER 3: TARGETS — -25%/-50%/-100% of Asian Range from band edge
        t25  = asian_edge + ar * 0.25 * bias
        t50  = asian_edge + ar * 0.50 * bias

DISTRIBUTION SYMMETRY TRAP — EXECUTION PROTOCOL
  PRE-SESSION (2:45 AM EST):
    Measure Asian Range (7PM-3AM) → Classify Tier
    Mark Asian High and Asian Low on chart
  LAYER 1 — BIAS LOCK (3AM-12PM):
    Wait for FIRST M5 CLOSE outside Asian band
    LONG bias if close > Asian High | SHORT bias if close < Asian Low
    This sets direction for the entire session — no second-guessing
  LAYER 2 — ATOMIC ENTRY (after bias lock):
    Wait for impulse candle in bias direction (body >= Atomic Unit x 0.5)
    Wait for NEXT candle to close OPPOSITE direction (pullback)
    Enter MARKET on pullback candle close
    Record entry for SL and target calculation
  LAYER 3 — TARGETS (position management):
    T25 = Band Extreme ± (AR x 0.25) → Close 50% of position
    T50 = Band Extreme ± (AR x 0.50) → Close 40% of position
    T100 = Band Extreme ± (AR x 1.00) → Close remaining 10% runner
    Move SL to breakeven after T25 hit
  INVALIDATION (81.2% RULE):
    SL = M5 CLOSE back inside Asian band — NOT wicks, CLOSES only
    If M5 closes inside: exit immediately, no holding for recovery
  HARD EXIT: 12:00 PM EST — close ALL positions regardless of target status
  POSITION SIZING:
    Risk = 0.25% equity per trade
    Size = (Equity x 0.0025) / (Atomic Unit in $)
    Max daily loss = 1.0% (4 losing trades x 0.25%)

THE INFINITE LADDER Distribution Harvesting Grid System Intraday |

April 2026
A grid system built on constraint resolution physics rather than random walk assumptions. High accuracy means drawdown is
temporary float, not risk. The grid pre-positions inside the known rebalancing zone. Distribution completes before the structural stop
is ever reached in 81-94% of activations.
THE CORE ASSUMPTION SHIFT Traditional grids fail because they assume random walk — so deeper entries
feel like averaging into losers. The Infinite Ladder pre-positions inside a statistically known partial rebalancing
zone [6] where the field [1] resolves its constraint deficit [11] before reversing past the structural boundary [8].
The float cost (temporary negative equity) is a premium collected back with interest when distribution
completes. TV and distribution are inverse — this is why traders struggle to achieve both high accuracy and high
frequency. With the atomic model, both are available simultaneously.
SECTION 1: THE HARVEST GRID — DAILY STRUCTURE
Front-load half the position at the band edge. Distribute the remainder across the density zone in equal increments. Close from the
top down as price runs toward the Atomic target. Let the deepest entries nest as free positions toward the shift target.

  At 10p (approaching Atomic target):

  The nested residual from each day becomes a compounding free position.

=== PAGE 147 ===
HEDGE RULE — CASCADE TIMING LOGIC:
  The temporal hedge on Mon/Tue follows the same cascade timing as the
  intraday P90 cascade (Part 14 loop-triggered protocol).
  Open opposite position at 12PM EST (session close).
  Target: 80% of the Atomic rebalancing range (not full reversal).
  Time exit: close at next session open (not 12PM next day).
  This captures the overnight/early morning rebalancing variance
  while the primary grid holds its positions through the night.
  The structural hedge (post-80% weekly fill) is simpler:
  Place the buy stop. Set the target. Walk away.
  If it fires: it monetizes the Thursday stall rebalance.
  If it doesn't: primary ran clean to distribution target.
  Either outcome is a win. No active management required.
SECTION 4: THE NESTING SYSTEM — MULTI-TIMEFRAME COMPOUNDING
The deepest grid entry from each day carries forward as a free position. After the macro anchor fires (Setup 5), every daily and
weekly grid becomes a compounding layer on the same monthly target. By Day 13-15 the position stack is massive — all at
near-zero cost basis.

=== PAGE 148 ===
MONTHLY NESTING TIMELINE:
  Day 1-5 (Week 1):
    Macro anchor fires (5-Day breakout + 2h hold)
    Monthly bias established
    Daily grids run → deepest entries nest as free positions
    Temporal hedges on Mon 12PM and Tue 12PM
  Day 6-10 (Week 2):
    Primary distribution run
    No hedge until 60% weekly target hit
    60% hit: stop adding, let existing grid ride
    60% to 80%: add aggressively (Wed-Thu momentum window)
    Each day's deepest entry nesting compounds the macro float
  Day 10-13 (Week 2-3 overlap):
    80% weekly fill: structural hedges placed (buy stops)
    Trim leading positions → secure capital advance
    Multiple nested residuals from each daily grid now running
    All at near-zero cost basis pointing at monthly 2.0x target
    The "heavy cloud" — three timeframes aligned at peak density
  Day 13-15 (Week 3):
    Macro target approaching (2.0x extension)
    Trim intraweek residuals → close 80% of nested positions
    Leave one position as the carry into next month if bias continues
  Day 15:
    Close macro + all nested positions
    Reset grid for new month
    Carry position (if monthly bias continues): stays open
    This carry is the "Day 1 free position" of the next month's grid
THE SCALE EFFECT:
  After a full month of nesting:
    Daily compounding: 15+ sessions of deepest entries stacking
    Weekly compounding: 3 complete weekly cycles adding residuals
    Macro compounding: one primary position running the full 2.0x
    Hedge income: temporal + structural variance captured throughout
  From the outside: looks like an overleveraged multi-position mess.
  From inside the model: a precision distribution harvesting machine
  with 81-94% accuracy on every layer and structural stops that almost
  never get hit because the field resolves before reaching them.
SECTION 5: DAILY OPERATING PLAYBOOK

=== PAGE 149 ===
PRE-3AM:
  Classify tier. Calculate density zone. Calculate weekly % completion.
  Place limit grid (front-load half at band edge, fill to -15p depth).
  Check distribution phase (which of the four phases applies today?).
3AM - 12PM (INTRADAY GRID):
  Grid fills as price runs through density zone.
  Close top entries as price approaches Atomic target (10/12/15p).
  At Atomic completion: close 50% of grid, let rest ride to shift target.
  Open temporal hedge at 12PM on Mon/Tue only.
12PM (DAILY CLOSE):
  Close all intraday entries EXCEPT deepest 1-2 (the nest).
  Nest positions stay open as long as macro bias holds.
  Record the day's grid performance.
WEEKLY PHASE CHECK (run each Wednesday):
  Where are we in the weekly distribution?
  Less than 60%: add aggressively Thursday
  60-80%: hold, watch for cloud formation, prepare structural hedges
  80%+: trim + place buy stop structural hedges
DAY 10 MACRO CHECK:
  Calculate regime ratio (Day 1-10 range / 5-Day anchor)
  CONFIRMED (>1.5x): hold to 2.0x target
  FAILED (<1.45x): trim aggressively, take 1.6x instead
  Note: regime check only adjusts size/target — grid structure unchanged
DAY 15:
  Close macro + trim nested positions to one carry.
  Reset. Start new month's grid.
SECTION 6: DISTRIBUTION TRACKER — PINE SCRIPT INDICATOR SPEC
Two simple trackers. No complex calculations. The model provides all the interpretation — the indicator just needs to surface the
percentage number.
Tracker 1 — Intraweek Distribution Completion
Parameter
Specification
Base
Monday Asian Range High/Low (from 7PM Sunday to 3AM Monday EST)
Factor
Tier base multiplier (T1: 3.12x | T2: 2.68x | T3: 2.18x) — applied to Monday Asian Range for weekly target
Calculation
Weekly Target = Monday Asian Range × Tier Factor
Live tracking
Current price distance from Monday Asian edge / Weekly Target = Completion %
Display
Single line: INTRAWEEK: XX.X% — updates live as price moves
No recalculation
Set Monday Asian Range at 3AM Monday. Fixed for the week. No adjustments.

THE FIB ALIGNMENT — WHY THE THRESHOLDS WORK

=== PAGE 153 ===
The 60% and 80% thresholds are not arbitrary percentages chosen for aesthetics.
They land on standard Fibonacci extension levels every time because the
constraint resolution multipliers (3.12x / 2.68x / 2.18x) were derived
from the same market data that produced the Fibonacci stall architecture.
T1 (3.12x): 60% of 3.12 = 1.872 ≈ 161.8% Fib extension
            80% of 3.12 = 2.496 ≈ 200% Fib extension (Deep State [10])
T2 (2.68x): 60% of 2.68 = 1.608 ≈ 138.2% Fib extension
            80% of 2.68 = 2.144 ≈ 161.8% Fib extension
T3 (2.18x): 60% of 2.18 = 1.308 ≈ 127.2% Fib extension
            80% of 2.18 = 1.744 ≈ 138.2% Fib extension
This means you have two confirmation systems pointing at the same level:
  → The distribution percentage tracker says "60% filled — enter Phase 2"
  → The Fib extension on your chart says "161.8% stall zone reached"
Both are the same physical event. That is why the 60/80 threshold grid
works mechanically across all three timeframes without adjustment.
The field resolves its constraint deficit through the same stall architecture
whether the reference window is 6 hours (daily), 5 days (intraweek),
or 5 weeks (macro). The physics scale. The Fibonacci levels stay the same.

Execution Code — ETH OCC Invalidation Test (Python)

=== PAGE 170 ===
import pandas as pd, numpy as np
def run_eth_occ_test(csv_path, buffers=[5,7,10]):
    df = pd.read_csv(csv_path, header=None,
        names=['dt','open','high','low',
               'close','vol','spread','ts'])
    df['dt'] = pd.to_datetime(df['dt'],
        format='%Y.%m.%d %H:%M:%S')
    df = df.set_index('dt').sort_index()
    df = (df.tz_localize('UTC') if df.index.tz
          is None else df).tz_convert(
          'America/New_York')
    df['session'] = (df.index -
        pd.Timedelta(hours=3)).dt.floor('D')
    res = {b:{'wr':[],'rm':[],'ws':0,'cs':0}
           for b in buffers}
    for day, data in df.groupby('session'):
        asian = data[(data.index.hour>=19)|
                     (data.index.hour<3)]
        if len(asian)<10: continue
        ar = asian['high'].max()-asian['low'].min()
        if ar<70:    t,au="T1",35
        elif ar<105: t,au="T2",42
        elif ar<160: t,au="T3",52
        else: continue
        sess = data[(data.index.hour>=3)&
                    (data.index.hour<12)]
        if sess.empty: continue
        state,orig,dir_='SEARCH',None,0
        for i,r in sess.iterrows():
          if state=='SEARCH':
            if orig is None: orig=r['close']
            up=r['high']-orig
            dn=orig-r['low']
            if up>=au and r['close']<r['open']:
              state,dir_,orig='PB',-1,r['high']
            elif dn>=au and r['close']>r['open']:
              state,dir_,orig='PB',1,r['low']
elif state=='PB':
            dz_l=(orig-au*1.2 if dir_==-1
                  else orig+au*0.8)
            dz_u=(orig-au*0.8 if dir_==-1
                  else orig+au*1.2)
            opp=((r['close']>r['open'])
                 if dir_==-1
                 else (r['close']<r['open']))
            if dz_l<=r['close']<=dz_u and opp:
              entry=r['close']
              occ=(r['high'] if dir_==-1
                   else r['low'])
              tgt=entry+(au*dir_)
              fut=sess[sess.index>i]
              if fut.empty:
                state,orig='SEARCH',None
                continue
              for buf in buffers:
                stop=occ+(buf*dir_)
                tp,cs,ws=False,False,False
                for _,f in fut.iterrows():
                  if ((dir_==1 and
                       f['close']>=tgt) or
                      (dir_==-1 and
                       f['close']<=tgt)):
                    tp=True; break
                  if ((dir_==1 and
                       f['close']<=stop) or
                      (dir_==-1 and
                       f['close']>=stop)):
                    cs=True; break
                  if ((dir_==1 and
                       f['low']<=stop) or
                      (dir_==-1 and
                       f['high']>=stop)):
                    ws=True
                if tp:
                  res[buf]['wr'].append(1)
                  rsk=abs(entry-stop)
                  rwd=abs(tgt-entry)
                  res[buf]['rm'].append(
                    rwd/rsk if rsk>0 else 0)
                elif cs:
                  res[buf]['wr'].append(0)
                  res[buf]['cs']+=1
                elif ws: res[buf]['ws']+=1
              state,orig='SEARCH',None
    return {b:{'hit_rate':
      round(np.mean(d['wr'])*100,1),
      'avg_r':round(np.mean(d['rm']),2)}
      for b,d in res.items()}
# RUN:

Execution Code — BTC Atomic Symmetry Trap (Python)
import pandas as pd, numpy as np
from datetime import timedelta
def run_btc_atomic_backtest(
        csv_path, buffer=30,
        risk_per_trade=0.0025):
    df = pd.read_csv(csv_path, header=None,
        names=['dt','open','high','low',
               'close','vol','spread','ts'])
    df['dt'] = pd.to_datetime(df['dt'],
        format='%Y.%m.%d %H:%M:%S')
    df = df.set_index('dt').sort_index()
    df = (df.tz_localize('UTC') if
        df.index.tz is None else df
        ).tz_convert('America/New_York')
    df['session'] = (df.index -
        timedelta(hours=3)).dt.floor('D')
    trades = []
    for day, data in df.groupby('session'):
        asian=data[(data.index.hour>=19)|
                   (data.index.hour<3)]
        if len(asian)<10: continue
        ar=asian['high'].max()-asian['low'].min()
        if ar<680:   tier,au="T1",240
        elif ar<1250:tier,au="T2",480
        elif ar<1950:tier,au="T3",820
        else: continue
        window=data[(data.index.hour>=3)&
                    (data.index.hour<12)]
        if window.empty: continue
        state,origin,dir_='SEARCH',None,0
        for i,r in window.iterrows():
          if state=='SEARCH':
            if origin is None: origin=r['close']
            up=r['high']-origin
            dn=origin-r['low']
            if up>=au and r['close']<r['open']:
              state,dir_,origin='PB',-1,r['high']
            elif dn>=au and r['close']>r['open']:
              state,dir_,origin='PB',1,r['low']
elif state=='PB':
            dz_l=(origin-au*1.20 if dir_==-1
                  else origin+au*0.80)
            dz_u=(origin-au*0.80 if dir_==-1
                  else origin+au*1.20)
            opp=((r['close']>r['open'])
                 if dir_==-1
                 else (r['close']<r['open']))
            if dz_l<=r['close']<=dz_u and opp:
              entry=r['close']
              occ=(r['high'] if dir_==-1
                   else r['low'])
              tgt=entry+(au*dir_)
              stop=(occ-buffer if dir_==1
                    else occ+buffer)
              risk=abs(entry-stop)
              rew=abs(tgt-entry)
              rw=rew/risk if risk>0 else 0
              fut=window[window.index>i]
              tp,sl,te=False,False,False
              xi=None
              for _,f in fut.iterrows():
                if f.index.hour>=12:
                  te=True; xi=f.index; break
                if dir_==1:
                  if f['close']>=tgt:
                    tp=True; xi=f.index; break
                  if f['close']<=stop:
                    sl=True; xi=f.index; break
                else:
                  if f['close']<=tgt:
                    tp=True; xi=f.index; break
                  if f['close']>=stop:
                    sl=True; xi=f.index; break
              rm=(rw if tp else
                  -1.0 if sl else 0.0)
              dur=((xi-i).total_seconds()/60
                   if xi else None)
              trades.append({'date':day,
                'tier':tier,'result':
                'WIN' if tp else
                'LOSS' if sl else 'TIME',
                'r_mult':rm,'dur':dur})
              state,origin='SEARCH',None
    return pd.DataFrame(trades)

#   'BTCUSD_M5.csv')

UNIVERSAL TIER DISCOVERY — K-MEANS METHODOLOGY
Every instrument below uses the same tier discovery process. Asian Range data from 7PM-3AM EST is clustered via k-means into 3 natural
volatility groups. Atomic Unit = ~50% of tier centroid. Tier Trigger = AU x 1.2. Density Zone = AU ±20%. These are structural parameters
derived from data — not assumed from forex scaling.
# UNIVERSAL TIER DISCOVERY TEMPLATE (run on any M5 CSV)
from sklearn.cluster import KMeans
import numpy as np, pandas as pd
def discover_tiers(csv_path, start='2024-01-01', end='2026-05-08', pip_size=0.0001):
    df = pd.read_csv(csv_path, header=None,
        names=['dt','open','high','low','close','vol','spread','ts'])
    df['dt'] = pd.to_datetime(df['dt'])
    df = df.set_index('dt').sort_index()
    df = df.tz_localize('UTC').tz_convert('America/New_York')
    df['session'] = (df.index - pd.Timedelta(hours=3)).dt.floor('D')
    ranges = []
    for day, data in df.groupby('session'):
        asian = data[(data.index.hour>=19)|(data.index.hour<3)]
        if len(asian) >= 10:
            ar = (asian['high'].max()-asian['low'].min())/pip_size
            ranges.append(ar)
    ranges = np.array(ranges).reshape(-1,1)
    km = KMeans(n_clusters=3, random_state=42, n_init=10).fit(ranges)
    c = sorted(km.cluster_centers_.flatten())
    return {
        'cutoffs': ((c[0]+c[1])/2, (c[1]+c[2])/2),
        'T1': {'au': int(c[0]*0.5), 'trig': int(c[0]*0.5*1.2)},
        'T2': {'au': int(c[1]*0.5), 'trig': int(c[1]*0.5*1.2)},
        'T3': {'au': int(c[2]*0.5), 'trig': int(c[2]*0.5*1.2)},
    }
# Apply to any instrument. Use recent-only data if regime expanded >12%.
TIGHT SL = DEFAULT ACROSS ALL NEW ASSETS. OCC Extreme with NO buffer (close-only invalidation). Pattern: WR drops ~2-5%
vs buffer baseline, R-multiple lifts +67-84%. Net expectancy gain: +56% to +84% across all instruments. Use buffer only on T3 days or
during high-impact news. All codes use 0.25% risk per trade.

OPTION B — CONTINUOUS LOOP SUPER SCALPER 3-5 Signals Per Session | All 19
Assets | Same Tier Parameters Cerebus Cycle — Final Form | April 2026
Option A (Atomic Symmetry Trap): one entry per session — Asian Bias Lock → first atomic signal → hold to 1 AU target. Option B
(Continuous Loop): same tier parameters, same entry logic, but the state machine resets after every trade exit (TP, SL, or 12PM), capturing
3-5 atomic loops per session instead of one. The 'First Impulse' filter in Option A was suppressing ~70% of valid atomic loops. Removing it
and running continuously restores full frequency while win rates hold above 82%.
THE ONLY DIFFERENCE BETWEEN OPTION A AND OPTION B: Option A stops after the first valid signal per session. Option B resets
the state machine after each exit and continues searching until 12PM EST. Same tier thresholds. Same Atomic Units. Same SL logic.
Same hard exit. Just more loops captured — 3-5x the trade frequency.

=== PAGE 188 ===
OPTION B — CONTINUOUS LOOP SUPER SCALPER
  SAME AS OPTION A EXCEPT:
    After each trade exit (TP, SL, or 12PM), state resets to SEARCH
    New origin = close price at exit
    Continue looking for next atomic loop until 12PM EST
    Maximum loops per session: 8 (cap to avoid noise)
  ENTRY (unchanged from Option A):
    Impulse >= Tier Trigger + body closes in opposite direction
    Enter on Opposite Candle Close inside Density Zone
  SL (unchanged):
    OCC Extreme + tier-specific buffer (close-only, wicks ignored)
  TARGET (unchanged):
    1 Atomic Unit from entry
    Close 100% at target
  HARD EXIT: 12:00 PM EST — close all, no exceptions
  RISK: 0.25% per trade (same as Option A)
  RECOMMENDED DEPLOYMENT:
    Option A: Pairs new to the model, higher-volatility crosses, crypto
    Option B: Proven assets (EUR, USD/CHF, indices) — maximizes frequency
    GBP crosses: Run Option B during T1/T2 days, Option A on T3 days
